Other measures tell different stories too. China held 4.31% of public repositories in the panel in early 2026 but only 0.91% of pushes. 9c:["$","p","7",{"children":[["$","$1","0",{"children":"The AI task studies do not measure national push growth. In a 2022 web-server experiment, participants who finished took an average of 161 minutes without Copilot and 71 minutes with it; three workplace trials involving 4,867 developers estimated about 26% more completed tasks with an assistant. A separate 2025 randomized study of 16 experienced developers in familiar open-source repositories found review-ready work, including tests and documentation, took 19% longer when AI tools were allowed. A February 2026 follow-up suggested possible savings with newer tools, but selection and time-measurement problems prevented a reliable estimate. The bibliography links to each study."}]]}]
9d:["$","p","8",{"children":[["$","$1","0",{"children":"The observational study drew on more than 500,000 GitHub developers across several AI tools. Its pooled synchronous-agent estimates compared adopters with similarly active developers observed a year earlier and accounted for changes in that comparison group. In weeks 21 to 30 after adoption, estimated weekly developer-attributed commits were 153% higher, projects contributed to were 47% higher, and weekly releases among recently worked-on repositories were 19.8% higher. Each outcome can have a different eligible sample, so the release estimate is not a fraction of the commit gain. The authors found more rewriting and branches, which they interpret as experimentation that can outpace reviewing and releasing. These are observational estimates, not a randomized experiment or a count of autonomous-agent output. "}],["$","a","1",{"href":"https://www.mertdemirer.com/Papers/Demirer_Writing_vs_shipping_code.pdf","children":"Writing Code vs. Shipping Code"}]]}]

A release record does not certify a successful product.
